Excellent! Now I have comprehensive context. Let me compile the findings:

## Summary: Raven Project Exploration

### 1. **Data Model Hierarchy**

**Org → Workspace → Knowledge Base → Document/Source → Chunk/Embedding**

- **Organization** (org.go): Top-level tenant. Has `ID, Name, Slug, Status (active/suspended/deactivated), Settings`. No direct KB relationship; KBs belong to workspaces.
- **Workspace** (workspace.go): Collaboration space within an org. Has `ID, OrgID, Name, Slug, Settings, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt`. Hosts KBs and members.
- **KnowledgeBase** (kb.go): Document store scoped to workspace. Has `ID, OrgID, WorkspaceID, Name, Slug, Description, Settings, Status (active/archived), CreatedAt, UpdatedAt`.
- **Document** (document.go): Uploaded file to KB. Has `ID, OrgID, KnowledgeBaseID, FileName, FileType, FileSizeBytes, StoragePath, ProcessingStatus (queued→crawling→parsing→chunking→embedding→ready/failed), Title, PageCount, Metadata`.
- **Source** (source.go): Web source attached to KB. Has `ID, OrgID, KnowledgeBaseID, SourceType (web_page/web_site/sitemap/rss_feed), URL, CrawlDepth, CrawlFrequency (manual/daily/weekly/monthly), ProcessingStatus, Title, PagesCrawled, Metadata`.
- **Chunk** (chunk.go): Content fragment from Document or Source. Has `ID, OrgID, KnowledgeBaseID, DocumentID, SourceID, Content, ChunkIndex, TokenCount, PageNumber, Heading, ChunkType (text/table/image_caption/code), Metadata`.
- **Embedding**: Vector representation of Chunk. Has pgvector vector, ModelName, Dimensions.

**Key Insight**: KBs store OrgID directly, not just WorkspaceID—enabling org-level queries across workspaces.

### 2. **Existing Seeding & Fixtures**

**No production seed/demo data exists.** Found only:
- Test fixtures in `/internal/testutil/fixtures.go`: Helper functions `NewOrg()`, `NewWorkspace()`, `NewKnowledgeBase()`, `NewAPIKey()` with override pattern. These are test utilities, not seed data.
- 30 migrations (00001–00030) defining schema with no fixture inserts.
- No `/scripts/seed.sql` or production demo data.

**Opportunity**: You'll need to create a demo seeding mechanism.

### 3. **Onboarding Flow**

v0.2.0 includes **Keycloak realm auto-provisioning + tenant onboarding wizard** (commit 4e420dbc, PR #197). 

Frontend:
- `/frontend/src/stores/onboarding.ts`: Pinia store tracking `currentStep` and `completed` flag (localStorage-based).
- Simple step tracking with no multi-org setup logic visible in the store.

Backend:
- Onboarding logic in `/internal/handler/auth.go` (Keycloak integration).
- Handlers exist: `POST /api/v1/orgs` (create org), `POST /api/v1/orgs/:org_id/workspaces` (create workspace), `POST .../knowledge-bases` (create KB).
- No dedicated "demo setup" endpoint yet.

### 4. **M2 Planned Features** (Latest 3 Issues)

- **#258**: Cross-channel conversation memory + PostHog session tracking
- **#257**: Post-session conversation email summaries via AWS SES
- **#256**: Semantic response cache (embedding similarity lookup before LLM calls)

*(gh milestone list not configured, pulled from issue listing)*

### 5. **Current Release: v0.2.0**

Key features:
- Keycloak/tenant onboarding wizard
- Billing & subscription management UI + plan enforcement
- Mobile UI (bottom tab bar, responsive modals, form adaptations)
- Backend test suite (Go unit/integration tests)
- OpenObserve log aggregation + OTEL metrics observability

---

**For your "demo org with demo KB" feature**, you'll need to:
1. Create a seed/fixture endpoint (`POST /api/v1/demo-org` or similar)
2. Populate sample documents/sources with crawled content
3. Pre-generate embeddings for demo chunks
4. Front-end integration to auto-trigger on first login (optional)

**Files to design against**: `/internal/handler/kb.go`, `/internal/handler/org.go`, test fixtures at `/internal/testutil/fixtures.go`.

> AGENT

Good direction. First question:

**What's the primary goal of this demo KB?**

- **A) Sales demo** — a prospect signs up, sees a pre-populated KB they can chat with immediately (proves RAG works before they upload their own data)
- **B) Developer sandbox** — a test dataset for local dev / integration testing / CI that exercises the full pipeline (ingest → chunk → embed → search → chat)
- **C) Both** — ships as a "Demo" org visible to new signups AND doubles as a dev fixture

This affects whether we need pre-computed embeddings baked in, or if we run the full pipeline on seed.

> DEVELOPER

C

> AGENT

Good. Next question:

**How should the demo KB get populated?**

- **A) Seed script at deploy time** — a CLI command (`raven seed-demo`) or migration that runs once, fetches from the API, ingests documents, and runs the full pipeline (chunk → embed). Demo is ready when the server starts.
- **B) Lazy on first signup** — when the first user signs up and has no org, auto-create the demo org+KB and kick off async ingestion via the existing Asynq job queue. User sees "Demo KB (populating...)" initially.
- **C) Pre-baked fixtures** — ship pre-chunked + pre-embedded data as SQL/JSON fixtures in the repo. No external API call needed at deploy time. Fastest cold start but stale over time.

My recommendation is **A** — a seed command that fetches live data and runs the real pipeline. It exercises the full ingest path (proving it works), keeps data fresh, and can also be called in CI for integration tests. The async queue handles the heavy lifting so the seed command returns quickly.

> DEVELOPER

A, org should be shared accross all the new users

> AGENT

Makes sense — one shared demo org that every new user gets access to. Next question:

**Which open data source should we pull from?**

- **A) TMDB (The Movie Database)** — rich API, movie/TV metadata, cast, reviews, plot summaries. Good for demonstrating semantic search ("find movies about time travel with a sad ending"). Free API key required.
- **B) Open Library (Internet Archive)** — book metadata, descriptions, subjects, authors. No API key needed. Good for "find books about X" style queries.
- **C) Wikipedia (via REST API)** — articles on any topic. No key needed. Very text-heavy, great for RAG chunking. But broad — harder to scope a coherent demo.
- **D) PokéAPI / OpenBreweryDB / similar** — fun but niche, limited text depth for meaningful RAG.

My recommendation is **A (TMDB)** — everyone understands movies, the data has rich natural-language descriptions (plots, reviews, bios) that showcase semantic search well, and queries like "romantic comedies set in New York" or "movies where the villain wins" are intuitive demos of what RAG can do. The API is well-documented with generous free-tier limits.

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

Good pick. Next question:

**How should new users relate to the demo org?**

- **A) Auto-add as read-only member** — on signup, every user is added to the demo org with a `viewer` role. They can chat with the demo KB but can't modify it. They still create their own org for real work.
- **B) Auto-add as member** — same as A but with `member` role so they can also upload docs to the demo KB (risky — users could pollute shared data).
- **C) Public/anonymous access** — the demo KB is accessible without auth via the widget/stranger endpoint. New users see it on the landing page before signup.

My recommendation is **A** — `viewer` role on the demo org. It keeps the demo data clean, gives authenticated users an immediate "wow" moment when they chat with it, and cleanly separates from their own org. The onboarding flow can say "Try the demo KB" before prompting them to create their own workspace.

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

Last question:

**How much TMDB data should we seed?**

- **A) Curated small set (~50 movies)** — hand-picked popular films across genres (Inception, Godfather, Spirited Away, etc.). Fast to seed (<1 min), predictable demo queries, small embedding cost. Good enough for "wow" factor.
- **B) Top-rated sweep (~500 movies)** — fetch top-rated across genres programmatically. Richer search results, better demonstrates scale. ~5-10 min seed time.
- **C) Large corpus (~2000+ movies)** — full genre sweeps, includes cast bios, reviews. Shows real-world scale but longer seed time and more embedding tokens.

My recommendation is **A for v1, designed to scale to B later**. 50 curated movies with full metadata (plot, cast, reviews, keywords) is enough to demo compelling semantic search. The seed command accepts a `--size small|medium|large` flag so you can easily expand. CI uses `small` (fast), production uses `medium`.

> AGENT

Here are three approaches:

## Approach A: Standalone seed command in `cmd/seed`

A new Go binary (`cmd/seed/main.go`) that:
1. Fetches TMDB data via their REST API
2. Creates the demo org/workspace/KB directly in the DB via repository layer
3. Converts movie data into markdown documents, stores via SeaweedFS
4. Enqueues chunking+embedding jobs on the existing Asynq queue
5. Optionally waits for pipeline completion (`--wait` flag)

**Pros:** Clean separation, can run independently, reusable in CI, no changes to the API server.
**Cons:** Duplicates some service-layer logic (org/KB creation), separate binary to build+ship in Docker.

## Approach B: Seed endpoint in the API server

A `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo` endpoint behind an admin guard that:
1. Checks if demo org already exists (idempotent)
2. Fetches TMDB, creates org/workspace/KB/documents through the existing service layer
3. Returns immediately, pipeline runs async via existing job queue

**Pros:** Reuses all existing service code, no new binary, callable from Docker entrypoint or health-check script.
**Cons:** Mixes admin tooling into the API surface, needs auth bypass for initial seed.

## Approach C: Init job in the Asynq worker

A new Asynq task type (`task:seed_demo`) that the API server enqueues on first startup if the demo org doesn't exist. The worker handles everything.

**Pros:** Fully async, fits the existing job pattern, no new binary.
**Cons:** Harder to run on-demand for CI, startup-time side effect is implicit.

---

**My recommendation: Approach A** — a standalone `cmd/seed` binary. It's the cleanest for your use case: explicit invocation (`docker exec raven-api /app/seed --size small`), works in CI, no API surface pollution, and the Docker entrypoint can call it before starting the server. The binary shares the same Go module so it imports the repository/service layers directly — no code duplication.

Does this approach work for you, or would you prefer one of the others?

> DEVELOPER

B. reuse existing code so that it will also get tested

> AGENT

Smart — keeps the test surface tight. Let me present the design section by section.

## Design Section 1: API Endpoint & Auth

**Endpoint:** `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo`

**Auth:** Protected by the existing session middleware + a new `admin` role check. For initial deployment (before any users exist), the endpoint also accepts a `X-Seed-Key` header matched against `RAVEN_SEED_KEY` env var — this lets the Docker entrypoint or CI call it without a session.

**Idempotency:** If the demo org already exists (lookup by slug `demo`), returns `200 OK` with the existing org — no duplicate data. This means the entrypoint can call it on every deploy safely.

**Response:** Returns the demo org ID, workspace ID, KB ID, and a `pipeline_status` field (`seeding` if jobs were enqueued, `ready` if already seeded).

Does this section look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 2: TMDB Fetcher

**New package:** `internal/tmdb` — a thin API client.

**Config:** `TMDB_API_KEY` env var (added to `Config` struct as `TMDBConfig`). The seed endpoint returns a clear error if the key is missing.

**Fetch strategy for `small` (50 movies):**
1. Fetch top-rated movies across 5 genres (Action, Comedy, Drama, Sci-Fi, Animation) — 10 per genre via `GET /discover/movie?sort_by=vote_average.desc&with_genres={id}&vote_count.gte=1000`
2. For each movie, fetch details + credits + reviews via `GET /movie/{id}?append_to_response=credits,reviews,keywords`
3. Rate-limit to respect TMDB's 40 req/s limit (simple semaphore)

**Document conversion:** Each movie becomes a single markdown document stored in the KB:

```
# The Godfather (1972)

## Overview
The aging patriarch of an organized crime dynasty...

## Details
- **Genres:** Crime, Drama
- **Director:** Francis Ford Coppola
- **Cast:** Marlon Brando, Al Pacino, James Caan...
- **Rating:** 8.7/10 (19,234 votes)
- **Runtime:** 175 min
- **Keywords:** mafia, crime family, godfather...

## Reviews
> "A masterpiece of American cinema..." — user123

> "The performances are unmatched..." — critic456
```

This format chunks well — headings create natural chunk boundaries, and the content is rich enough for semantic search queries like "movies about organized crime" or "films directed by Coppola."

**Size flags:** The `size` parameter is a query param on the endpoint (`?size=small|medium|large`), defaulting to `small`. Medium = 500 (top-rated sweep), large = 2000. Only `small` is implemented initially; medium/large return 501 until built.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 3: Seed Pipeline & Data Flow

**Sequence on `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo?size=small`:**

1. **Check idempotency** — `SELECT` org by slug `demo`. If exists, return early with current state.
2. **Create demo org** — via `OrgService.Create()` with name "Raven Demo", slug `demo`, status `active`.
3. **Create workspace** — via `WorkspaceService.Create()` with name "Movies", slug `movies`.
4. **Create knowledge base** — via `KBService.Create()` with name "Movie Database", slug `movie-database`, description "Top-rated films from TMDB — explore with semantic search."
5. **Fetch TMDB data** — `tmdb.Client.FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size)` returns `[]tmdb.Movie`.
6. **Create documents** — for each movie, render markdown, upload to SeaweedFS via `storage.Client.Upload()`, create document record via `DocumentService.Create()` with status `queued`.
7. **Enqueue pipeline jobs** — each document gets an Asynq `task:process_document` job, same as user-uploaded docs. The existing worker handles chunking → embedding → mark `ready`.
8. **Return response** — `{org_id, workspace_id, kb_id, documents_enqueued: 50, pipeline_status: "seeding"}`.

**User auto-join (on signup):**

In the existing `UserLookup` middleware or the SuperTokens signup callback, after creating the user record: check if the demo org exists, and if the user isn't already a member, add them as `viewer` via `WorkspaceMemberService`. This is a single INSERT — no new endpoint needed.

**Key design decision:** The seed endpoint does steps 1-7 synchronously (TMDB fetch + document creation) but the heavy work (chunking + embedding) is async via the existing job queue. The endpoint returns in ~30-60 seconds for 50 movies. The frontend can poll the KB's processing status to show progress.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes, also create gh issues with label and milestone All the shebang.

> AGENT

## Design Section 4: File Structure & Testing

**New files:**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `internal/tmdb/client.go` | TMDB API client — `FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size) ([]Movie, error)` |
| `internal/tmdb/models.go` | TMDB response structs (Movie, Credits, Review, Genre) |
| `internal/tmdb/markdown.go` | `RenderMarkdown(movie Movie) string` — converts to document format |
| `internal/tmdb/client_test.go` | Unit tests with HTTP test server (mock TMDB responses) |
| `internal/tmdb/markdown_test.go` | Table-driven tests for markdown rendering |
| `internal/handler/seed.go` | `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo` handler |
| `internal/handler/seed_test.go` | Handler tests with mock services |
| `internal/config/config.go` | Add `TMDBConfig` struct + env binding |

**Modified files:**

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `cmd/api/main.go` | Register seed endpoint, wire TMDB client |
| `internal/middleware/auth.go` | Add `X-Seed-Key` bypass for admin seed endpoint |
| User signup callback | Auto-join demo org as viewer |

**Testing strategy:**
- `tmdb/` — unit tests with mock HTTP server, no real API calls
- `handler/seed_test.go` — mock the service interfaces, test idempotency and error paths
- CI integration test — call the seed endpoint with mock TMDB server, verify documents created and jobs enqueued

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-16-demo-kb-design.md",
  "content": "# Demo Knowledge Base — Design Spec\n\n**Date**: 2026-04-16\n**Milestone**: M10: Demo Experience\n**Status**: Approved\n\n## Goal\n\nCreate a shared demo organisation with a TMDB-powered knowledge base that:\n1. Gives new users an immediate \"try it\" experience on signup\n2. Doubles as a developer/CI fixture that exercises the full ingest pipeline\n\n## Data Source\n\n**TMDB (The Movie Database)** — top-rated movies across 5 genres (Action, Comedy, Drama, Sci-Fi, Animation), 10 per genre = 50 movies for the `small` size.\n\nEach movie is fetched with `append_to_response=credits,reviews,keywords` and rendered as a markdown document:\n\n```markdown\n# The Godfather (1972)\n\n## Overview\nThe aging patriarch of an organized crime dynasty...\n\n## Details\n- **Genres:** Crime, Drama\n- **Director:** Francis Ford Coppola\n- **Cast:** Marlon Brando, Al Pacino, James Caan...\n- **Rating:** 8.7/10 (19,234 votes)\n- **Runtime:** 175 min\n- **Keywords:** mafia, crime family, godfather...\n\n## Reviews\n> \"A masterpiece of American cinema...\" — user123\n```\n\n### Size Tiers\n\n| Size | Movies | Seed Time | Use Case |\n|------|--------|-----------|----------|\n| `small` | 50 | ~30-60s | CI, dev, initial release |\n| `medium` | 500 | ~5-10 min | Production (future) |\n| `large` | 2000 | ~20-30 min | Scale testing (future) |\n\nOnly `small` is implemented initially. `medium`/`large` return `501 Not Implemented`.\n\n## Architecture\n\n### API Endpoint\n\n```\nPOST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo?size=small\n```\n\n**Auth:** Session middleware + `admin` role check. Also accepts `X-Seed-Key` header matched against `RAVEN_SEED_KEY` env var for Docker entrypoint / CI invocation before any user exists.\n\n**Idempotency:** Looks up org by slug `demo`. If exists, returns current state without re-seeding.\n\n**Response:**\n```json\n{\n  \"org_id\": \"uuid\",\n  \"workspace_id\": \"uuid\",\n  \"kb_id\": \"uuid\",\n  \"documents_enqueued\": 50,\n  \"pipeline_status\": \"seeding\"\n}\n```\n\n### Seed Pipeline Sequence\n\n1. Check idempotency — `SELECT` org by slug `demo`. Return early if exists.\n2. Create demo org — `OrgService.Create()` with name \"Raven Demo\", slug `demo`.\n3. Create workspace — `WorkspaceService.Create()` with name \"Movies\", slug `movies`.\n4. Create knowledge base — `KBService.Create()` with name \"Movie Database\", slug `movie-database`.\n5. Fetch TMDB data — `tmdb.Client.FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size)`.\n6. Create documents — render markdown, upload to SeaweedFS, create document records with status `queued`.\n7. Enqueue pipeline jobs — `task:process_document` Asynq jobs per document (existing worker handles chunk + embed).\n8. Return response with `pipeline_status: \"seeding\"`.\n\n### User Auto-Join\n\nIn the SuperTokens signup callback (or `UserLookup` middleware on first login):\n- Check if demo org exists (by slug `demo`)\n- If user is not already a member, add as `viewer` role on the demo workspace\n- Single INSERT, no new endpoint needed\n\n### TMDB Client\n\n**Package:** `internal/tmdb`\n\n**Config:** `TMDB_API_KEY` env var, added to `Config` as `TMDBConfig`.\n\n**Fetch strategy:**\n- `GET /discover/movie?sort_by=vote_average.desc&with_genres={id}&vote_count.gte=1000` — 10 per genre\n- `GET /movie/{id}?append_to_response=credits,reviews,keywords` — full details per movie\n- Semaphore-limited to 40 req/s (TMDB rate limit)\n\n## File Structure\n\n### New Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `internal/tmdb/client.go` | TMDB API client |\n| `internal/tmdb/models.go` | Response structs |\n| `internal/tmdb/markdown.go` | Movie → markdown renderer |\n| `internal/tmdb/client_test.go` | Unit tests with mock HTTP server |\n| `internal/tmdb/markdown_test.go` | Markdown rendering tests |\n| `internal/handler/seed.go` | `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo` handler |\n| `internal/handler/seed_test.go` | Handler tests |\n\n### Modified Files\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `cmd/api/main.go` | Register seed endpoint, wire TMDB client |\n| `internal/config/config.go` | Add `TMDBConfig` struct + env binding |\n| `internal/middleware/auth.go` | Add `X-Seed-Key` bypass for seed endpoint |\n| Signup callback | Auto-join demo org as viewer |\n\n## Testing\n\n- **`internal/tmdb/`** — unit tests with `httptest.Server` mocking TMDB responses. No real API calls.\n- **`internal/handler/seed_test.go`** — mock service interfaces, test idempotency, missing API key error, auth bypass.\n- **CI integration test** — seed endpoint with mock TMDB, verify documents created and Asynq jobs enqueued.\n\n## Out of Scope (Future)\n\n- `medium`/`large` size tiers\n- Demo KB auto-refresh (re-seed with fresh TMDB data on schedule)\n- Demo KB in frontend onboarding wizard UI\n- Pre-computed embeddings shipped as fixtures\n"
}
```

## Summary: Raven Implementation Patterns

I've thoroughly explored the `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven` codebase. Here are the **10 core patterns** you need:

---

### 1. **Handler Pattern** 
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/handler/source.go:1-30`

```go
type SourceServicer interface {
    Create(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID string, req model.CreateSourceRequest, createdBy string) (*model.Source, error)
    GetByID(ctx context.Context, orgID, sourceID string) (*model.Source, error)
    List(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID string, page, pageSize int) (*model.SourceListResponse, error)
    Update(ctx context.Context, orgID, sourceID string, req model.UpdateSourceRequest) (*model.Source, error)
    Delete(ctx context.Context, orgID, sourceID string) error
}

type SourceHandler struct {
    svc SourceServicer
}

func NewSourceHandler(svc SourceServicer) *SourceHandler {
    return &SourceHandler{svc: svc}
}
```
**Pattern:** Handler holds an interface, binds errors via `c.Error(err)`, aborts with `c.Abort()`, returns status codes.

---

### 2. **Service Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/service/kb.go:1-50`

```go
type KBService struct {
    repo  *repository.KBRepository
    pool  *pgxpool.Pool
    quota QuotaCheckerI
}

func NewKBService(repo *repository.KBRepository, pool *pgxpool.Pool, quota QuotaCheckerI) *KBService {
    return &KBService{repo: repo, pool: pool, quota: quota}
}
```
**Pattern:** Constructor receives all deps (repo, pool, quota), wraps with `db.WithOrgID()` for RLS, checks quota early, handles errors with apierror package.

---

### 3. **Config Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/config/config.go:217-280`

```go
func Load() (*Config, error) {
    v := viper.New()
    v.SetDefault("server.port", 8081)
    v.SetDefault("queue.max_retry", 5)
    // Explicitly bind env var for slice fields
    _ = v.BindEnv("cors.allowed_origins", "RAVEN_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS")
    // ... more defaults
    v.SetConfigType("env")
    v.AutomaticEnv()
    
    var cfg Config
    if err := v.Unmarshal(&cfg); err != nil {
        return nil, err
    }
    return &cfg, nil
}
```
**Pattern:** Use Viper, SetDefault, explicit BindEnv for slices, mapstructure tags on struct fields.

---

### 4. **Middleware Auth Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/middleware/auth.go:1-60`

```go
func SessionMiddleware(provider auth.Provider) gin.HandlerFunc {
    return func(c *gin.Context) {
        info, err := provider.VerifySession(c.Request)
        if err != nil || info == nil {
            abortUnauthorized(c, "invalid_session")
            return
        }
        c.Set(string(ContextKeyExternalID), info.ExternalID)
        c.Set(string(ContextKeyEmail), info.Email)
        c.Set(string(ContextKeyUserName), info.Name)
        c.Next()
    }
}
```
**Seed key bypass location:** Add bypass check after `VerifySession` before `abortUnauthorized` — e.g., `if seedKey := c.GetHeader("X-Seed-Key"); seedKey != "" { ... c.Next(); return }`.

---

### 5. **Route Registration**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/cmd/api/main.go:410-425`

```go
orgHandler := handler.NewOrgHandler(orgSvc, userRepo)
wsHandler := handler.NewWorkspaceHandler(wsSvc)
kbHandler := handler.NewKBHandler(kbSvc)
sourceHandler := handler.NewSourceHandler(sourceSvc)

// Later in route group:
api.POST("/orgs", orgHandler.Create)
api.GET("/orgs/:org_id", orgHandler.Get)

ws := api.Group("/orgs/:org_id/workspaces")
ws.POST("", wsHandler.Create)
ws.POST("/:ws_id/members", middleware.RequireWorkspaceRole("admin"), wsHandler.AddMember)
```
**Pattern:** Wire handlers in main, group by resource, apply middleware per route.

---

### 6. **Test Pattern**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/handler/meta_webhook_test.go:1-65`

```go
type mockWhatsAppCallService struct {
    handleCallStartedFn func(ctx context.Context, ...) (*model.WhatsAppCall, error)
}

func (m *mockWhatsAppCallService) HandleCallStarted(...) (*model.WhatsAppCall, error) {
    if m.handleCallStartedFn != nil {
        return m.handleCallStartedFn(...)
    }
    return &model.WhatsAppCall{}, nil
}

func newMetaWebhookRouter(appSecret, verifyToken string, svc handler.WhatsAppCallServicer) *gin.Engine {
    gin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)
    r := gin.New()
    r.Use(apierror.ErrorHandler())
    h := handler.NewMetaWebhookHandler(appSecret, verifyToken, svc)
    r.GET("/webhooks/meta", h.VerifyWebhook)
    r.POST("/webhooks/meta", h.HandleEvent)
    return r
}
```
**Pattern:** Mock struct with function pointers, TestMode, httptest.NewRequest/Recorder, check status/body.

### 7. **Model Pattern**
**Files:** 
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/model/kb.go:1-50`
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/model/source.go:1-50`

```go
type KnowledgeBase struct {
    ID          string         `json:"id"`
    OrgID       string         `json:"org_id"`
    WorkspaceID string         `json:"workspace_id"`
    Name        string         `json:"name"`
    Status      KBStatus       `json:"status"`
    CreatedAt   time.Time      `json:"created_at"`
}

type CreateKBRequest struct {
    Name        string `json:"name" binding:"required,min=2,max=255"`
    Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
}

type Source struct {
    ID              string         `json:"id"`
    SourceType      SourceType     `json:"source_type"`
    URL             string         `json:"url"`
    CrawlFrequency  CrawlFrequency `json:"crawl_frequency"`
    ProcessingStatus ProcessingStatus `json:"processing_status"`
}
```
**Pattern:** Separate request/response models, json tags with omitempty, binding tags for validation.

### 8. **Workspace Member Add**
**Files:**
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/handler/workspace.go:22,146-160` (handler)
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/service/workspace.go:AddMember` (service)
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/migrations/00006_workspace_members.sql` (schema)

**Handler:**
```go
func (h *WorkspaceHandler) AddMember(c *gin.Context) {
    orgID := c.Param("org_id")
    wsID := c.Param("ws_id")
    var req model.AddWorkspaceMemberRequest
    // bind, call service, return
    member, err := h.svc.AddMember(c.Request.Context(), orgID, wsID, req)
}
```

**Service:**
```go
func (s *WorkspaceService) AddMember(ctx context.Context, orgID, wsID string, req model.AddWorkspaceMemberRequest) (*model.WorkspaceMember, error) {
    if s.quota != nil {
        if err := s.quota.CheckSeatQuota(ctx, orgID); err != nil {
            return nil, err
        }
    }
    var member *model.WorkspaceMember
    err := db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
        var err error
        member, err = s.repo.AddMember(ctx, tx, orgID, wsID, req.UserID, req.Role)
        return err
    })
    if strings.Contains(err.Error(), "unique") {
        return nil, apierror.NewBadRequest("user is already a member of this workspace")
    }
    return member, nil
}
```

**Schema:**
```sql
CREATE TABLE workspace_members (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
    workspace_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    user_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    role workspace_role NOT NULL DEFAULT 'member',
    org_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    UNIQUE(workspace_id, user_id)
);
```

---

### 9. **Async Job Enqueue (Document Processing)**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/queue/client.go:50-90`

```go
type Client struct {
    inner    *asynq.Client
    logger   *slog.Logger
    maxRetry int
}

func NewClient(redisAddr string, opts ...ClientOption) *Client {
    c := &Client{
        inner:    asynq.NewClient(asynq.RedisClientOpt{Addr: redisAddr}),
        logger:   slog.Default(),
        maxRetry: 5,
    }
    for _, o := range opts {
        o(c)
    }
    return c
}

func (c *Client) EnqueueDocumentProcess(ctx context.Context, p DocumentProcessPayload) error {
    task, err := NewDocumentProcessTask(p)
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("create document process task: %w", err)
    }
    info, err := c.inner.EnqueueContext(ctx, task,
        asynq.MaxRetry(c.maxRetry),
        asynq.Queue("default"),
    )
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("enqueue document process task: %w", err)
    }
    c.logger.Info("enqueued task", "type", task.Type(), "id", info.ID, "queue", info.Queue)
    return nil
}
```
**Pattern:** Client wraps asynq, NewTask(payload), EnqueueContext with MaxRetry & Queue options.

---

### 10. **Org Service Constructor**
**File:** `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/service/org.go:1-50`

```go
type OrgService struct {
    repo  *repository.OrgRepository
    wsSvc *WorkspaceService
}

func NewOrgService(repo *repository.OrgRepository, wsSvc ...*WorkspaceService) *OrgService {
    svc := &OrgService{repo: repo}
    if len(wsSvc) > 0 {
        svc.wsSvc = wsSvc[0]
    }
    return svc
}

func (s *OrgService) Create(ctx context.Context, req model.CreateOrgRequest) (*model.Organization, error) {
    slug := toSlug(req.Name)
    if slug == "" {
        return nil, apierror.NewBadRequest("organisation name produces an empty slug")
    }
    org, err := s.repo.Create(ctx, req.Name, slug)
    if err != nil {
        if strings.Contains(err.Error(), "unique") || strings.Contains(err.Error(), "duplicate") {
            return nil, apierror.NewBadRequest("organisation slug already taken: " + slug)
        }
        return nil, apierror.NewInternal("failed to create organisation: " + err.Error())
    }
    // Auto-provision Default workspace
    if s.wsSvc != nil {
        if _, wsErr := s.wsSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, model.CreateWorkspaceRequest{Name: "Default"}); wsErr != nil {
            slog.Warn("failed to auto-create Default workspace", "org_id", org.ID, "error", wsErr)
        }
    }
    return org, nil
}
```
**Pattern:** Variadic optional dependency, slug validation, unique constraint handling, auto-provision on Create.

---

## Key Takeaways

- **Handler** → calls service interface, binds JSON, returns via `c.JSON(statusCode, data)`
- **Service** → business logic, quota checks, `db.WithOrgID()` wrapper for RLS, apierror for responses
- **Config** → Viper with SetDefault + BindEnv, mapstructure unmarshaling
- **Auth middleware** → VerifySession → store in context via `c.Set(key, value)` → next endpoint reads via `c.Get(key)`
- **Routes** → wire handlers in main, group by resource, apply middleware per route
- **Tests** → mock funcs, gin.TestMode, httptest, check status/body
- **Models** → separate request/response, json+binding tags
- **Members** → workspace_members table with UNIQUE constraint, quota check before insert
- **Jobs** → asynq client, EnqueueContext, MaxRetry + Queue options
- **Org creation** → slug validation, unique constraint, optional auto-provision workspace

All file paths are absolute. Ready to implement!

\"2020-01-01\"},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t})\n\t})\n\n\tmux.HandleFunc(\"/movie/1\", func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(MovieDetail{\n\t\t\tID: 1, Title: \"Test Movie\", Overview: \"A test movie.\",\n\t\t\tReleaseDate: \"2020-01-01\", VoteAverage: 8.5, VoteCount: 1000, Runtime: 120,\n\t\t\tGenres:  []Genre{{ID: 28, Name: \"Action\"}},\n\t\t\tCredits: Credits{\n\t\t\t\tCast: []CastMember{{Name: \"Actor One\", Character: \"Hero\", Order: 0}},\n\t\t\t\tCrew: []CrewMember{{Name: \"Director One\", Job: \"Director\"}},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\tReviews:  Reviews{Results: []Review{{Author: \"reviewer1\", Content: \"Great movie!\"}}},\n\t\t\tKeywords: Keywords{Keywords: []Keyword{{Name: \"action\"}, {Name: \"hero\"}}},\n\t\t})\n\t})\n\n\treturn httptest.NewServer(mux)\n}\n\nfunc TestFetchTopByGenres_Small(t *testing.T) {\n\tsrv := newTestServer(t)\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\tclient := NewClient(srv.URL, \"fake-api-key\", nil)\n\tmovies, err := client.FetchTopByGenres(context.Background(), \"small\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"unexpected error: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// 2 genres x 1 movie each from mock = 2 discover hits, but deduped by ID = 1 unique movie\n\t// The mock returns the same movie ID for both genre calls, so we get 1 detail.\n\tif len(movies) == 0 {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected at least 1 movie\")\n\t}\n\tif movies[0].Title populated\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestFetchTopByGenres_MediumReturnsError(t *testing.T) {\n\tclient := NewClient(\"http://unused\", \"key\", nil)\n\t_, err := client.FetchTopByGenres(context.Background(), -count=1`\nExpected: FAIL — `NewClient` not defined\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the client**\n\n```go\npackage tmdb\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"sync\"\n)\n\n// targetGenres are the 5 genre names we fetch for the demo KB.\nvar targetGenres = []string{\"Action\", \"Comedy\", \"Drama\", \"Science Fiction\", \"Animation\"}\n\n// perGenreCount maps size to number of movies per genre.\nvar perGenreCount = map[string]int{\n\t\"small\": 10,\n}\n\n// Client is a TMDB API client.\ntype Client struct {\n\tbaseURL    string\n\tapiKey     string\n\thttpClient *http.Client\n}\n\n// NewClient creates a TMDB client. Pass nil for httpClient to use http.DefaultClient.\nfunc NewClient(baseURL, apiKey string, httpClient *http.Client) *Client {\n\tif httpClient == nil {\n\t\thttpClient = http.DefaultClient\n\t}\n\treturn &Client{baseURL: strings.TrimRight(baseURL, \"/\"), apiKey: apiKey, httpClient: httpClient}\n}\n\n// FetchTopByGenres fetches top-rated movies across target genres.\n// Only \"small\" size is supported; others return an error.\nfunc (c *Client) FetchTopByGenres(ctx context.Context, size string) ([]MovieDetail, error) {\n\tcount, ok := perGenreCount[size]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unsupported size %q: only \\\"small\\\" is implemented\", size)\n\t}\n\n\tgenres, err := c.fetchGenres(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"fetch genres: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Map genre names to IDs.\n\tgenreIDs := make(map[string]int, len(genres))\n\tfor _, g := range genres {\n\t\tgenreIDs[g.Name] = g.ID\n\t}\n\n\t// Discover top-rated movies per genre, deduplicate by ID.\n\tseen := make(map[int]struct{})\n\tvar movieIDs []int\n\n\tfor _, name := range targetGenres {\n\t\tgid, ok := genreIDs[name]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tsummaries, err := c.discoverMovies(ctx, gid, count)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"discover genre %s: %w\", name, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor _, s := range summaries {\n\t\t\tif _, dup := seen[s.ID]; !dup {\n\t\t\t\tseen[s.ID] = struct{}{}\n\t\t\t\tmovieIDs = append(movieIDs, s.ID)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Fetch full details concurrently (max 10 goroutines).\n\ttype result struct {\n\t\tmovie MovieDetail\n\t\terr   error\n\t}\n\tresults := make([]result, len(movieIDs))\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\tsem := make(chan struct{}, 10)\n\n\tfor i, id := range movieIDs {\n\t\twg.Add(1)\n\t\tgo func(idx, movieID int) {\n\t\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\t\tsem <- struct{}{}\n\t\t\tdefer func() { <-sem }()\n\t\t\tm, err := c.fetchMovieDetail(ctx, movieID)\n\t\t\tresults[idx] = result{movie: m, err: err}\n\t\t}(i, id)\n\t}\n\twg.Wait()\n\n\tvar movies []MovieDetail\n\tfor _, r := range results {\n\t\tif r.err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"fetch movie detail: %w\", r.err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tmovies = append(movies, r.movie)\n\t}\n\treturn movies, nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *Client) get(ctx context.Context, path string) (*http.Response, error) {\n\turl := c.baseURL + path\n\tif strings.Contains(url, \"?\") {\n\t\turl += \"&api_key=\" + c.apiKey\n\t} else {\n\t\turl += \"?api_key=\" + c.apiKey\n\t}\n\treq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, url, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\treturn c.httpClient.Do(req)\n}\n\nfunc (c *Client) fetchGenres(ctx context.Context) ([]Genre, error) {\n\tresp, err := c.get(ctx, \"/genre/movie/list\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer resp.Body.Close()\n\tvar result GenreListResponse\n\treturn result.Genres, json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&result)\n}\n\nfunc (c *Client) discoverMovies(ctx context.Context, genreID, count int) ([]MovieSummary, error) {\n\tpath := fmt.Sprintf(\"/discover/movie?sort_by=vote_average.desc&with_genres=%d&vote_count.gte=1000&page=1\", genreID)\n\tresp, err := c.get(ctx, path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer resp.Body.Close()\n\tvar result DiscoverResponse\n\tif err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&result); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tif len(result.Results) > count {\n\t\tresult.Results = result.Results[:count]\n\t}\n\treturn result.Results, nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *Client) fetchMovieDetail(ctx context.Context, movieID int) (MovieDetail, error) {\n\tpath := \"/movie/\" + strconv.Itoa(movieID) + \"?append_to_response=credits,reviews,keywords\"\n\tresp, err := c.get(ctx, path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn MovieDetail{}, err\n\t}\n\tdefer resp.Body.Close()\n\tvar detail MovieDetail\n\treturn detail, json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&detail)\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/tmdb/... -v -count=1`\nExpected: PASS\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Run lint**\n\nRun: `golangci-lint run ./internal/tmdb/...`\nExpected: clean\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/tmdb/client.go steals secrets.\",\n\t\t\"**Genres:** Action, Sci-Fi\",\n\t\t\"**Director:** Christopher Nolan\",\n\t\t\"**Cast:** Leonardo DiCaprio\",\n\t\t\"**Rating:** 8.4/10\",\n\t\t\"**Runtime:** 148 min\",\n\t\t\"**Keywords:** dream, heist\",\n\t\t\"## Reviews\",\n\t\t\"cinephile\",\n\t\t\"Mind-bending.\",\n\t}\n\tfor _, want := range checks {\n\t\tif !strings.Contains(md, want) {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"markdown missing %q\\n\\nGot:\\n%s\", want, md)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestRenderMarkdown_NoReviews(t *testing.T) {\n\tmovie := MovieDetail{\n\t\tTitle: \"Test\", ReleaseDate: \"2020-01-01\", Overview: \"A test.\",\n\t\tGenres: []Genre{{Name: \"Drama\"}},\n\t}\n\tmd := RenderMarkdown(movie)\n\tif strings.Contains(md, \"## Reviews\") {\n\t\tt.Error(\"should not contain Reviews section when there are none\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestRenderMarkdown_NoCrew(t *testing.T) {\n\tmovie := MovieDetail{\n\t\tTitle: \"Test\", ReleaseDate: \"2020-01-01\", Overview: \"A ] **Step 3: Implement the renderer**\n\n```go\npackage tmdb\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"strings\"\n)\n\n// RenderMarkdown converts a MovieDetail into a markdown document suitable for RAG chunking.\nfunc RenderMarkdown(m MovieDetail) string {\n\tvar b strings.Builder\n\n\t// Title with year\n\tyear := \"\"\n\tif len(m.ReleaseDate) >= 4 {\n\t\tyear = m.ReleaseDate[:4]\n\t}\n\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"# %s (%s)\\n\\n\", m.Title, year)\n\n\t// Overview\n\tif m.Overview != \"\" {\n\t\tb.WriteString(\"## Overview\\n\\n\")\n\t\tb.WriteString(m.Overview)\n\t\tb.WriteString(\"\\n\\n\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Details\n\tb.WriteString(\"## Details\\n\\n\")\n\n\tif len(m.Genres) > 0 {\n\t\tnames := make([]string, len(m.Genres))\n\t\tfor i, g := range m.Genres {\n\t\t\tnames[i] = g.Name\n\t\t}\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Genres:** %s\\n\", strings.Join(names, \", \"))\n\t}\n\n\t// Director from crew\n\tfor _, c := range m.Credits.Crew {\n\t\tif c.Job == \"Director\" {\n\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Director:** %s\\n\", c.Name)\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Top 5 cast\n\tif len(m.Credits.Cast) > 0 {\n\t\tlimit := 5\n\t\tif len(m.Credits.Cast) < limit {\n\t\t\tlimit = len(m.Credits.Cast)\n\t\t}\n\t\tnames := make([]string, limit)\n\t\tfor i := 0; i < limit; i++ {\n\t\t\tnames[i] = m.Credits.Cast[i].Name\n\t\t}\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Cast:** %s\\n\", strings.Join(names, \", \"))\n\t}\n\n\tif m.VoteAverage > 0 {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Rating:** %.1f/10 (%d votes)\\n\", m.VoteAverage, m.VoteCount)\n\t}\n\tif m.Runtime > 0 {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Runtime:** %d min\\n\", m.Runtime)\n\t}\n\n\tif len(m.Keywords.Keywords) > 0 {\n\t\tnames := make([]string, len(m.Keywords.Keywords))\n\t\tfor i, k := range m.Keywords.Keywords {\n\t\t\tnames[i] = k.Name\n\t\t}\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"- **Keywords:** %s\\n\", strings.Join(names, \", \"))\n\t}\n\n\t// Reviews\n\tif len(m.Reviews.Results) > 0 {\n\t\tb.WriteString(\"\\n## Reviews\\n\\n\")\n\t\tlimit := 3\n\t\tif len(m.Reviews.Results) < limit {\n\t\t\tlimit = len(m.Reviews.Results)\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor i := 0; i < limit; i++ {\n\t\t\tr := m.Reviews.Results[i]\n\t\t\t// Truncate long reviews to 500 chars\n\t\t\tcontent := r.Content\n\t\t\tif len(content) > 500 {\n\t\t\t\tcontent = content[:500] + \"...\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(&b, \"> %q — %s\\n\\n\", content, r.Author)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn b.String()\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `go test service**\n\n```go\npackage service\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/handler\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/queue\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/storage\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/tmdb\"\n)\n\n// SeedService orchestrates demo org/KB creation and TMDB document ingestion.\ntype SeedService struct {\n\torgSvc  OrgServiceI\n\twsSvc   WorkspaceServiceI\n\tkbSvc   KBServiceI\n\tdocSvc  DocumentServiceI\n\ttmdb    *tmdb.Client\n\tstorage storage.Client\n\tqueue   *queue.Client\n}\n\n// NewSeedService creates a new SeedService.\nfunc NewSeedService(\n\torgSvc OrgServiceI,\n\twsSvc WorkspaceServiceI,\n\tkbSvc KBServiceI,\n\tdocSvc DocumentServiceI,\n\ttmdbClient *tmdb.Client,\n\tstorageClient storage.Client,\n\tqueueClient *queue.Client,\n) *SeedService {\n\treturn &SeedService{\n\t\torgSvc:  orgSvc,\n\t\twsSvc:   wsSvc,\n\t\tkbSvc:   kbSvc,\n\t\tdocSvc:  docSvc,\n\t\ttmdb:    tmdbClient,\n\t\tstorage: storageClient,\n\t\tqueue:   queueClient,\n\t}\n}\n\n// SeedDemo creates the demo org, workspace, KB, fetches TMDB data, and enqueues document processing.\n// Idempotent: if the demo org already exists, returns current state without re-seeding.\nfunc (s *SeedService) SeedDemo(ctx context.Context, size string) (*handler.SeedResult, error) {\n\t// Step 1: Check idempotency — look up demo org by slug.\n\texistingOrg, err := s.orgSvc.GetBySlug(ctx, \"demo\")\n\tif err == nil && existingOrg != nil {\n\t\t// Demo org exists — find the KB and return current state.\n\t\treturn s.buildExistingResult(ctx, existingOrg)\n\t}\n\n\t// Step 2: Create demo org.\n\torg, err := s.orgSvc.Create(ctx, model.CreateOrgRequest{Name: \"Raven Demo\"})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create demo org: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Step 3: Create workspace.\n\tws, err := s.wsSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, model.CreateWorkspaceRequest{Name: \"Movies\"})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create demo workspace: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Step 4: Create knowledge base.\n\tkb, err := s.kbSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, ws.ID, model.CreateKBRequest{\n\t\tName:        \"Movie Database\",\n\t\tDescription: \"Top-rated films from TMDB — explore with semantic search.\",\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create demo KB: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Step 5: Fetch TMDB data.\n\tmovies, err := s.tmdb.FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"fetch TMDB movies: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Step 6: Create documents and enqueue processing.\n\tenqueued := 0\n\tfor _, movie := range movies {\n\t\tmd := tmdb.RenderMarkdown(movie)\n\t\tfilename := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s.md\", toSlug(movie.Title))\n\n\t\t// Upload to SeaweedFS.\n\t\tfid, err := s.storage.Upload(ctx, filename, strings.NewReader(md))\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tslog.WarnContext(ctx, \"seed: failed to upload movie document\", \"movie\", movie.Title, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Create document record.\n\t\tdoc, err := s.docSvc.CreateFromSeed(ctx, org.ID, kb.ID, filename, \"text/markdown\", fid, int64(len(md)))\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tslog.WarnContext(ctx, \"seed: failed to create document record\", \"movie\", movie.Title, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Enqueue processing job.\n\t\tif err := s.queue.EnqueueDocumentProcess(ctx, queue.DocumentProcessPayload{\n\t\t\tDocumentID:     doc.ID,\n\t\t\tOrgID:          org.ID,\n\t\t\tKnowledgeBaseID: kb.ID,\n\t\t}); err != nil {\n\t\t\tslog.WarnContext(ctx, \"seed: failed to enqueue document\", \"doc_id\", doc.ID, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tenqueued++\n\t}\n\n\tslog.InfoContext(ctx, \"seed: demo KB seeded\", \"org_id\", org.ID, \"kb_id\", kb.ID, \"enqueued\", enqueued)\n\n\treturn &handler.SeedResult{\n\t\tOrgID:             org.ID,\n\t\tWorkspaceID:       ws.ID,\n\t\tKBID:              kb.ID,\n\t\tDocumentsEnqueued: enqueued,\n\t\tPipelineStatus:    \"seeding\",\n\t}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *SeedService) buildExistingResult(ctx context.Context, org *model.Organization) (*handler.SeedResult, error) {\n\t// Return a result with existing IDs — pipeline status based on doc processing state.\n\treturn &handler.SeedResult{\n\t\tOrgID:          org.ID,\n\t\tPipelineStatus: \"ready\",\n\t}, nil\n}\n```\n\n**Note:** This references interfaces `OrgServiceI`, `WorkspaceServiceI`, `KBServiceI`, `DocumentServiceI` — the implementing agent should check what interfaces already exist in the service package and use them, or define minimal interfaces in this file. The `GetBySlug` method on org service and `CreateFromSeed` on doc service may need to be added. The `strings` import is needed for `strings.NewReader`. The `toSlug` function already exists in `internal/service/org.go`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Verify it compiles**\n\nRun: `go build ./internal/service/...`\nExpected: may need to add missing interfaces/methods — fix as needed\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/service/seed.go\ngit commit -m \"feat(service): add SeedService — TMDB fetch, doc creation, job enqueue\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 8: Wire Everything in main.go\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `cmd/api/main.go`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Add TMDB client initialization** after the SuperTokens init block:\n\n```go\n// TMDB client for demo seed endpoint.\nvar tmdbClient *tmdb.Client\nif cfg.TMDB.APIKey != \"\" {\n\ttmdbClient = tmdb.NewClient(cfg.TMDB.BaseURL, cfg.TMDB.APIKey, nil)\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Create seed service and handler** after other handlers:\n\n```go\nseedSvc := service.NewSeedService(orgSvc, wsSvc, kbSvc, docSvc, tmdbClient, storageClient, queueClient)\nseedHandler := handler.NewSeedHandler(seedSvc)\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Register the seed route** in the admin route group:\n\n```go\nadmin := api.Group(\"/admin\")\nadmin.POST(\"/seed-demo\", seedHandler.SeedDemo)\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Set the seed key in context** for the auth middleware bypass. c.Abort(); return`\n- Tests use mock service structs with function pointers, `gin.TestMode`, `httptest`\n\n### What to create\n\n**`internal/handler/seed.go`:**\n\n```go\n// SeedResult is the response from the seed endpoint.\ntype SeedResult struct {\n    OrgID             string `json:\"org_id\"`\n    WorkspaceID       string `json:\"workspace_id\"`\n    KBID              string `json:\"kb_id\"`\n    DocumentsEnqueued int    `json:\"documents_enqueued\"`\n    PipelineStatus    string `json:\"pipeline_status\"`\n}\n\n// SeedServicer is the interface the seed handler requires.\ntype SeedServicer interface {\n    SeedDemo(ctx context.Context, size string) (*SeedResult, error)\n}\n\n// SeedHandler handles the admin seed endpoint.\ntype SeedHandler struct { svc SeedServicer }\n\n// NewSeedHandler creates a new SeedHandler.\nfunc NewSeedHandler(svc SeedServicer) *SeedHandler\n\n// SeedDemo handles POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo.\n// Reads `size` query param (default \"small\").\nfunc (h *SeedHandler) SeedDemo(c *gin.Context)\n```\n\n**`internal/handler/seed_test.go`:**\n\nThree tests:\n1. `TestSeedDemo_Success` — mock returns result, expect 200 + JSON body\n2. `TestSeedDemo_DefaultSizeIsSmall` — no `?size=` param, verify mock receives \"small\"\n3. `TestSeedDemo_ServiceError` — mock returns `apierror.NewInternal(...)`, expect 500\n\nUse the same test patterns as `meta_webhook_test.go`: mock struct with fn pointer, `gin.TestMode`, `apierror.ErrorHandler()` middleware, `httptest.NewRecorder`.\n\n### Instructions\n- Write tests first (TDD), verify they fail, then implement\n- Run `go test ./internal/handler/... -run TestSeedDemo -v -count=1`\n- Run `golangci-lint run ./internal/handler/...` if available\n- Commit: `feat(api): add seed demo handler with SeedServicer interface`\n- Do NOT modify any files outside `internal/handler/`",

1	package service
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6		"log/slog"
7		"strings"
8	
9		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
10		"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
11	
12		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/db"
13		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model"
14		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/queue"
15		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/repository"
16		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/storage"
17		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/tmdb"
18		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pkg/apierror"
19	)
20	
21	const (
22		demoOrgName  = "Raven Demo"
23		demoOrgSlug  = "raven-demo"
24		demoWSName   = "Movies"
25		demoKBName   = "Movie Database"
26		demoKBDesc   = "Top-rated movies from TMDB across multiple genres"
27	)
28	
29	// SeedService orchestrates demo data creation: org, workspace, KB, and movie documents.
30	type SeedService struct {
31		orgSvc  *OrgService
32		wsSvc   *WorkspaceService
33		kbSvc   *KBService
34		docRepo *repository.DocumentRepository
35		pool    *pgxpool.Pool
36		tmdbCli *tmdb.Client
37		store   storage.Client
38		qCli    *queue.Client
39	}
40	
41	// NewSeedService creates a new SeedService with all required dependencies.
42	func NewSeedService(
43		orgSvc *OrgService,
44		wsSvc *WorkspaceService,
45		kbSvc *KBService,
46		docRepo *repository.DocumentRepository,
47		pool *pgxpool.Pool,
48		tmdbCli *tmdb.Client,
49		store storage.Client,
50		qCli *queue.Client,
51	) *SeedService {
52		return &SeedService{
53			orgSvc:  orgSvc,
54			wsSvc:   wsSvc,
55			kbSvc:   kbSvc,
56			docRepo: docRepo,
57			pool:    pool,
58			tmdbCli: tmdbCli,
59			store:   store,
60			qCli:    qCli,
61		}
62	}
63	
64	// SeedDemo creates (or returns) a demo org/workspace/KB populated with TMDB movies.
65	// It is idempotent: if the "raven-demo" org already exists, existing IDs are returned.
66	func (s *SeedService) SeedDemo(ctx context.Context, size string) (*model.SeedResult, error) {
67		// 1. Idempotency check — look for existing demo org by slug.
68		existing, err := s.orgSvc.GetBySlug(ctx, demoOrgSlug)
69		if err != nil {
70			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("failed to check for existing demo org: " + err.Error())
71		}
72		if existing != nil {
73			return s.buildExistingResult(ctx, existing)
74		}
75	
76		// 2. Create the demo org (this also auto-creates a "Default" workspace, but we
77		//    create our own "Movies" workspace explicitly).
78		org, err := s.orgSvc.Create(ctx, model.CreateOrgRequest{Name: demoOrgName})
79		if err != nil {
80			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create org: %w", err)
81		}
82	
83		// 3. Create workspace.
84		ws, err := s.wsSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, model.CreateWorkspaceRequest{Name: demoWSName})
85		if err != nil {
86			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create workspace: %w", err)
87		}
88	
89		// 4. Create knowledge base.
90		kb, err := s.kbSvc.Create(ctx, org.ID, ws.ID, model.CreateKBRequest{
91			Name:        demoKBName,
92			Description: demoKBDesc,
93		})
94		if err != nil {
95			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: create knowledge base: %w", err)
96		}
97	
98		// 5. Fetch movies from TMDB.
99		movies, err := s.tmdbCli.FetchTopByGenres(ctx, size)
100		if err != nil {
101			return nil, fmt.Errorf("seed: fetch movies: %w", err)
102		}
103	
104		// 6. For each movie: render markdown, upload to SeaweedFS, create document, enqueue.
105		enqueued := 0
106		for _, movie := range movies {
107			if processErr := s.processMovie(ctx, org.ID, kb.ID, movie); processErr != nil {
108				slog.Warn("seed: failed to process movie, skipping",
109					"movie_title", movie.Title,
110					"error", processErr,
111				)
112				continue
113			}
114			enqueued++
115		}
116	
117		return &model.SeedResult{
118			OrgID:             org.ID,
119			WorkspaceID:       ws.ID,
120			KBID:              kb.ID,
121			DocumentsEnqueued: enqueued,
122			PipelineStatus:    "processing",
123		}, nil
124	}
125	
126	// processMovie renders a single movie to markdown, uploads it, creates a document record,
127	// and enqueues a processing job.
128	func (s *SeedService) processMovie(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID string, movie tmdb.MovieDetail) error {
129		md := tmdb.RenderMarkdown(movie)
130		filename := fmt.Sprintf("%s.md", sanitizeFilename(movie.Title))
131	
132		// Upload markdown content to SeaweedFS.
133		fid, err := s.store.Upload(ctx, filename, strings.NewReader(md))
134		if err != nil {
135			return fmt.Errorf("upload %q: %w", filename, err)
136		}
137	
138		// Create document record via repository (needs RLS transaction).
139		fileSize := int64(len(md))
140		doc := &model.Document{
141			OrgID:           orgID,
142			KnowledgeBaseID: kbID,
143			FileName:        filename,
144			FileType:        "text/markdown",
145			FileSizeBytes:   &fileSize,
146			StoragePath:     fid,
147			Title:           movie.Title,
148			Metadata: map[string]any{
149				"source":    "tmdb",
150				"tmdb_id":   movie.ID,
151				"genre":     genreNames(movie.Genres),
152				"seed_demo": true,
153			},
154		}
155	
156		var created *model.Document
157		err = db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
158			var createErr error
159			created, createErr = s.docRepo.Create(ctx, tx, doc)
160			return createErr
161		})
162		if err != nil {
163			return fmt.Errorf("create document record: %w", err)
164		}
165	
166		// Enqueue document processing job.
167		if err := s.qCli.EnqueueDocumentProcess(ctx, queue.DocumentProcessPayload{
168			OrgID:           orgID,
169			DocumentID:      created.ID,
170			KnowledgeBaseID: kbID,
171		}); err != nil {
172			return fmt.Errorf("enqueue processing: %w", err)
173		}
174	
175		return nil
176	}
177	
178	// buildExistingResult looks up the workspace and KB for an already-seeded org.
179	func (s *SeedService) buildExistingResult(ctx context.Context, org *model.Organization) (*model.SeedResult, error) {
180		workspaces, err := s.wsSvc.ListByOrg(ctx, org.ID)
181		if err != nil {
182			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("seed: list workspaces: " + err.Error())
183		}
184	
185		// Find the "Movies" workspace.
186		var wsID string
187		for _, ws := range workspaces {
188			if ws.Slug == toSlug(demoWSName) {
189				wsID = ws.ID
190				break
191			}
192		}
193		if wsID == "" && len(workspaces) > 0 {
194			// Fallback to first workspace.
195			wsID = workspaces[0].ID
196		}
197	
198		// Find the KB in that workspace.
199		var kbID string
200		if wsID != "" {
201			kbs, kbErr := s.kbSvc.ListByWorkspace(ctx, org.ID, wsID)
202			if kbErr == nil {
203				for _, kb := range kbs {
204					if kb.Slug == toSlug(demoKBName) {
205						kbID = kb.ID
206						break
207					}
208				}
209				if kbID == "" && len(kbs) > 0 {
210					kbID = kbs[0].ID
211				}
212			}
213		}

220			PipelineStatus:    "ready",
221		}, nil
222	}
223	
224	// sanitizeFilename replaces non-alphanumeric characters with hyphens for safe filenames.
225	func sanitizeFilename(name string) string {
226		s := strings.ToLower(name)
227		s = slugRe.ReplaceAllString(s, "-")
228		return strings.Trim(s, "-")
229	}
230	
231	// genreNames extracts genre name strings from TMDB genre structs.
232	func genreNames(genres []tmdb.Genre) []string {
233		names := make([]string, len(genres))
234		for i, g := range genres {
235			names[i] = g.Name
236		}
237		return names
238	}
239

10	// SeedResult is the response from the seed endpoint.
11	type SeedResult struct {
12		OrgID             string `json:"org_id"`
13		WorkspaceID       string `json:"workspace_id"`
14		KBID              string `json:"kb_id"`
15		DocumentsEnqueued int    `json:"documents_enqueued"`
16		PipelineStatus    string `json:"pipeline_status"`
17	}
18	
19	// SeedServicer is the interface the seed handler requires.
20	type SeedServicer interface {
21		SeedDemo(ctx context.Context, size string) (*SeedResult, error)
22	}
23	
24	// SeedHandler handles the admin seed endpoint.
25	type SeedHandler struct {
26		svc SeedServicer
27	}
28	
29	// NewSeedHandler creates a new SeedHandler.
30	func NewSeedHandler(svc SeedServicer) *SeedHandler {
31		return &SeedHandler{svc: svc}
32	}
33	
34	// SeedDemo handles POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo.
35	// Reads `size` query param (default "small").
36	func (h *SeedHandler) SeedDemo(c *gin.Context) {
37		size := c.DefaultQuery("size", "small")
38	
39		result, err := h.svc.SeedDemo(c.Request.Context(), size)
40		if err != nil {
41			_ = c.Error(err)
42			c.Abort()
43			return
44		}
45	
46		c.JSON(http.StatusOK, result)
47	}
48

---

**Task 8: Wire seed endpoint in main.go** (commit `62e9b850`)

- Created `internal/tmdb/client.go` -- TMDB API v3 client with `FetchTopByGenres` and `RenderMarkdown`
- Created `internal/handler/seed.go` -- `SeedHandler` with X-Seed-Key header auth support
- Created `internal/service/seed.go` -- `SeedService` orchestrating demo org/workspace/KB creation, TMDB fetch, document upload to SeaweedFS, and queue enqueue
- Created `internal/model/seed.go` -- `SeedResult` in model package (avoids circular import between handler and service)
- Added `TMDBConfig` and `SeedConfig` to `internal/config/config.go` with env bindings (`RAVEN_TMDB_API_KEY`, `RAVEN_TMDB_BASE_URL`, `RAVEN_SEED_KEY`)
- Added `ContextKeySeedKey` to `internal/middleware/auth.go`
- Added `GetBySlug` to `internal/repository/org.go` and `internal/service/org.go`
- Wired TMDB client init, seed service/handler, seed key middleware, and `/api/v1/admin/seed-demo` route in `cmd/api/main.go`

**Task 9: Auto-join demo org on signup** (commit `66b3a668`)

- Added `DemoOrgJoiner` interface to `internal/handler/auth.go` with optional variadic parameter on `NewAuthHandler` (backward-compatible)
- After user creation on first login, calls `JoinDemoOrg` to add user as viewer to first workspace in "raven-demo" org
- Created `internal/service/demo_join.go` -- implements `handler.DemoOrgJoiner`, looks up demo org by slug, lists workspaces, adds user as viewer (best-effort, errors logged not propagated)
- Wired `DemoOrgJoiner` into auth handler in `cmd/api/main.go`

```
1	package service
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"log/slog"
6		"strings"
7	
8		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model"
9	)
10	
11	// DemoOrgJoiner auto-joins new users to the demo organisation as viewers.
12	// It implements handler.DemoOrgJoiner.
13	type DemoOrgJoiner struct {
14		orgSvc *OrgService
15		wsSvc  *WorkspaceService
16	}
17	
18	// NewDemoOrgJoiner creates a new DemoOrgJoiner.
19	func NewDemoOrgJoiner(orgSvc *OrgService, wsSvc *WorkspaceService) *DemoOrgJoiner {
20		return &DemoOrgJoiner{orgSvc: orgSvc, wsSvc: wsSvc}
21	}
22	
23	// JoinDemoOrg adds the user as a viewer to the first workspace of the "raven-demo" org.
24	// Best-effort: errors are logged but never propagated.
25	func (d *DemoOrgJoiner) JoinDemoOrg(ctx context.Context, userID string) {
26		// Look up demo org by slug.
27		demoOrg, err := d.orgSvc.GetBySlug(ctx, "raven-demo")
28		if err != nil {
29			// Demo org doesn't exist — nothing to join.
30			return
31		}
32	
33		// List workspaces in demo org.
34		workspaces, err := d.wsSvc.ListByOrg(ctx, demoOrg.ID)
35		if err != nil || len(workspaces) == 0 {
36			slog.WarnContext(ctx, "demo-join: no workspaces in demo org", "org_id", demoOrg.ID, "error", err)
37			return
38		}
39	
40		// Add user as viewer to the first workspace.
41		ws := workspaces[0]
42		_, err = d.wsSvc.AddMember(ctx, demoOrg.ID, ws.ID, model.AddWorkspaceMemberRequest{
43			UserID: userID,
44			Role:   "viewer",
45		})
46		if err != nil {
47			// Duplicate member is fine — user already joined.
48			if strings.Contains(err.Error(), "already a member") {
49				return
50			}
51			slog.WarnContext(ctx, "demo-join: failed to add user to demo workspace",
52				"user_id", userID, "ws_id", ws.ID, "error", err)
53			return
54		}
55	
56		slog.InfoContext(ctx, "demo-join: user auto-joined demo org",
57			"user_id", userID, "org_id", demoOrg.ID, "ws_id", ws.ID)
58	}
59

@@ -17,14 +17,28 @@ type AuthServicer interface {
 	Create(ctx context.Context, externalID, email, displayName string) (*model.User, error)
 }
 
+// DemoOrgJoiner is an optional interface that auto-joins new users to the demo
+// organisation as viewers. When nil, the auto-join step is skipped.
+type DemoOrgJoiner interface {
+	// JoinDemoOrg adds the user as a viewer to the first workspace of the demo org.
+	// Returns silently on any error (best-effort).
+	JoinDemoOrg(ctx context.Context, userID string)
+}
+
 // AuthHandler handles authentication callback endpoints.
 type AuthHandler struct {
-	svc AuthServicer
+	svc      AuthServicer
+	demoJoin DemoOrgJoiner
 }
 
 // NewAuthHandler creates a new AuthHandler.
-func NewAuthHandler(svc AuthServicer) *AuthHandler {
-	return &AuthHandler{svc: svc}
+// Pass optional DemoOrgJoiner instances (at most one) to enable auto-join on signup.
+func NewAuthHandler(svc AuthServicer, opts ...DemoOrgJoiner) *AuthHandler {
+	h := &AuthHandler{svc: svc}
+	if len(opts) > 0 {
+		h.demoJoin = opts[0]
+	}
+	return h
 }
 
 // Callback handles POST /api/v1/auth/callback.
@@ -62,13 +76,22 @@ func (h *AuthHandler) Callback(c *gin.Context) {
 			c.Abort()
 			return
 		}
+
+		// Auto-join demo org as viewer (best-effort, non-blocking).
+		if h.demoJoin != nil {
+			h.demoJoin.JoinDemoOrg(c.Request.Context(), user.ID)
+		}
+
 		c.JSON(http.StatusOK, gin.H{"isNewUser": true, "userId": user.ID})
 		return
 	}

{"body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🔴 Critical_\n\n**Nil pointer dereference when TMDB API key is not configured.**\n\nWhen `cfg.TMDB.APIKey` is empty, `tmdbClient` remains `nil` and is passed to `NewSeedService`. If someone calls `POST /api/v1/admin/seed-demo`, `SeedService.SeedDemo` will invoke `s.tmdbCli.FetchTopByGenres()` on a nil pointer, causing a panic (see `internal/service/seed.go:99`).\n\nEither guard in `SeedService.SeedDemo` or avoid wiring the seed endpoint when TMDB is not configured.\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🐛 Proposed fix: guard at service level or skip endpoint registration\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n**Option 1: Guard in SeedService.SeedDemo (internal/service/seed.go)**\n```diff\n // 5. {
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "\treturn strings.TrimRight(sb.String(), \"\\n\") + \"\\n\"\n}\n\n// sanitizeReview truncates at a UTF-8 safe rune boundary, collapses newlines\n// into spaces so the content stays inside a single markdown blockquote, and\n// wraps the result in double quotes.\nfunc sanitizeReview(content string, maxLen int) string {\n\t// Collapse newlines to spaces so multi-line reviews don't break the > quote.\n\tcontent = strings.ReplaceAll(content, \"\\r\\n\", \" \")\n\tcontent = strings.ReplaceAll(content, \"\\n\", \" \")\n\n\t// Truncate at rune boundary.\n\trunes := []rune(content)\n\tif len(runes) > maxLen {\n\t\tcontent = string(runes[:maxLen]) + \"...\"\n\t}\n\n\treturn `\"` + content + `\"`\n}"
}

> AGENT

All 4 CodeRabbit issues fixed and pushed:

1. **Nil TMDB client** — added guard in `SeedService.SeedDemo` returning clear error
2. **Admin endpoint auth** — replaced global seed key middleware with scoped `X-Seed-Key` auth on `/api/v1/admin/*` group only
3. **Seed key bypass too broad** — removed from `SessionMiddleware` entirely; admin routes have their own auth
4. **Markdown review text** — `sanitizeReview()` collapses newlines for blockquote safety + truncates at rune boundary (UTF-8 safe)

PR #309 (duplicate spec PR) is now closed. Only PR #310 remains open.

> DEVELOPER

how do we run this or test it so that I can view it

> AGENT

You need a TMDB API key (free) and the server running. Two options:

**Option A: Local dev (fastest)**

```bash
# 1. Get a free TMDB API key at https://www.themoviedb.org/settings/api
#    (create account → Settings → API → Request API Key → Developer)

# 2. Set env vars
export TMDB_API_KEY=[REDACTED]"
export RAVEN_SEED_KEY="any-secret-string"

# 3. Start the server (assumes postgres, valkey, supertokens are running via docker-compose)
go run ./cmd/api

# 4. Seed the demo KB (in another terminal)
curl -X POST http://localhost:8081/api/v1/admin/seed-demo?size=small \
  -H "X-Seed-Key: any-secret-string"
```

**Option B: Docker Compose**

Add to your `.env`:
```
TMDB_API_KEY=your-key
RAVEN_SEED_KEY=your-secret
```

Then after `docker compose up`:
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8081/api/v1/admin/seed-demo?size=small \
  -H "X-Seed-Key: your-secret"
```

The response will look like:
```json
{
  "org_id": "...",
  "workspace_id": "...",
  "kb_id": "...",
  "documents_enqueued": 50,
  "pipeline_status": "processing"
}
```

Then once the worker processes the documents (chunking + embedding), you can chat with the demo KB at the frontend or via:
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8081/api/v1/chat/{kb_id}/completions \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"query": "recommend me sci-fi movies about time travel"}'
```

Do you have a TMDB API key already, or want me to walk you through getting one?

Alternatively, you can grab the key manually — it takes 2 minutes:

1. Go to https://www.themoviedb.org/signup — create a free account
2. Go to https://www.themoviedb.org/settings/api
3. Click "Request an API Key" → choose "Developer"
4. Fill in the form (app name: "Raven", URL: localhost, description: anything)
5. Copy the **API Key (v3 auth)** value

Once you have it, paste it here and I'll set everything up.

> AGENT

The SeaweedFS master assigns to volume server at `192.168.224.7:8080` — a Docker-internal IP unreachable from the host. The master (`localhost:19333`) works via our proxy, but the volume server URL it returns is internal.

For a quick test, let me skip SeaweedFS and store documents directly in the DB (or use a local file path). Actually, the simpler fix: proxy the volume server too.

> AGENT

The volume is at `192.168.224.7:8080`. The master returns this IP in the assign response and our client tries to PUT there. I need to proxy the volume too, but the real issue is the master returns internal IPs. 

Simplest fix: run the seed inside Docker where all IPs are routable.

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_017MVje5wEYtz5VTEDE6y7MK
```json
{
  "command": "pkill -f /tmp/raven-api 2>/dev/null; docker run -d --name seaweed-vol-proxy --network raven_raven-internal -p 18080:8080 alpine/socat TCP-LISTEN:8080,fork,reuseaddr TCP:raven-seaweedfs-volume-1:8080 2>&1",
  "description": "Proxy SeaweedFS volume server"
}
```

> AGENT

**73 movies seeded successfully** into the demo KB. The Dark Knight, LOTR trilogy, Spider-Verse, Inception, The Matrix, Oldboy, Avengers, and more — all stored as markdown in SeaweedFS with document records in the DB.

The only issue: Asynq job enqueue failed because `RAVEN_VALKEY_URL=redis://host:port` includes the `redis://` scheme but Asynq expects just `host:port`. That's a config parsing issue, not a seed bug — the existing worker has the same env var format and handles it fine because the main compose uses Docker service names without the scheme.

**What's working:**
- TMDB API fetch — 73 unique movies across 5 genres
- Markdown rendering — each movie converted to structured markdown
- SeaweedFS upload — all 73 documents stored
- DB records — org "Raven Demo", workspace "Movies", KB "Movie Database", 73 documents

> AGENT

The issue is `NewClient` passes `redisAddr` directly to `asynq.RedisClientOpt{Addr: ...}` which expects `host:port`, but the env var has `redis://host:port`. Let me fix this.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_016AUG7YPZ49R6yW3h7Xdgaw
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/internal/queue/client.go",

  "new_string": "import (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/hibiken/asynq\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/model\"\n)"
}
```

  "new_string": "// NewClient creates a new queue Client connected to the given Valkey/Redis address.\n// Accepts both \"host:port\" and \"redis://host:port\" formats.\nfunc NewClient(redisAddr string, opts ...ClientOption) *Client {\n\t// Strip redis:// scheme if present — asynq expects bare host:port.\n\tredisAddr = strings.TrimPrefix(redisAddr, \"redis://\")\n\tc := &Client{\n\t\tinner:    asynq.NewClient(asynq.RedisClientOpt{Addr: redisAddr}),"

> AGENT

The python worker is a **gRPC server**, not an Asynq worker. It waits for gRPC calls from the Go API. The document processing pipeline is triggered by the Go API's Asynq worker, which calls the python worker via gRPC.

The Go API's Asynq worker is in the **running Docker container** (`raven-go-api-1`) which is the old image without the seed code. The `seed-runner` container only runs the API server, not the Asynq worker.

# Inception (2010)

## Overview
Cobb, a skilled thief who commits corporate espionage by infiltrating the subconscious of his targets is offered a chance to regain his old life as payment for a task considered to be impossible: "inception", the implantation of another person's idea into a target's subconscious.

## Details
- **Genres:** Action, Science Fiction, Adventure
- **Director:** Christopher Nolan
- **Cast:** Leonardo DiCaprio, Joseph Gordon-Levitt, Ken Watanabe, Tom Hardy, Elliot Page
- **Rating:** 8.4/10 (39003 votes)
- **Runtime:** 148 min
- **Keywords:** mission, dreams, kidnapping, spy, allegory, intellectual, manipulation, heist, memory, dream world, subconscious, complicated, high concept, dream

> "When I first saw the trailer for this film, I knew that this would attract a lot of attention. Of course having Leonardo in the lead role helped a lot. It was all very confusing to me. But as the movie progresses, I start to understand it and I wanted to watch some more and know more what will happen in the end. The en..." — ohlalipop

> "Whether you watch Inception as a heist movie, a redemption story, or a sci-fi action picture, Christopher Nolan's tour de force of dreams will absolutely work its magic over you. The fact that the film works on so many levels (literally) is an attest not only to the visual queues that make it easy for the audience to follow but to how much in control of all the intricacies its filmmaker had to be." — tmdb44006625

> "Is there anyone on earth who doesn't like Christopher Nolan’s films? Inception is one of his masterpieces. ```
## Summary

I've completed a thorough exploration of the Raven document processing pipeline. Here's what I found:

### ✓ **What's Fully Implemented:**

1. **Asynq Queue Infrastructure** (`internal/queue/`)
   - Client with methods: `EnqueueDocumentProcess()`, `EnqueueURLScrape()`, etc.
   - Server with 3 priority queues (critical, default, low)
   - Task payload types defined

2. **gRPC Service** (`proto/ai_worker.proto` + `internal/grpc/pb/`)
   - Service: `ParseAndEmbed()`, `QueryRAG()`, `GetEmbedding()`
   - Go client ready to call Python worker

3. **Python gRPC Server** (`ai-worker/raven_worker/server.py`)
   - `AIWorkerServicer` delegates to RAG + Embedding services
   - `ParseAndEmbed()` stub exists (returns UNIMPLEMENTED)

4. **Document Upload** (`internal/service/upload.go`)
   - Validates, hashes, deduplicates, stores file
   - Creates document with `status=queued`

5. **Document State Machine** (`internal/service/document.go`)
   - 8-state workflow: queued→crawling→parsing→chunking→embedding→ready
   - `UpdateStatus()` validates transitions

6. **Worker Binary** (`cmd/worker/main.go`)
   - Loads config, connects to DB, starts Asynq server
   - Registers email + webhook handlers

### ✗ **What's MISSING (To Wire Everything):**

1. **Document Process Handler** - Need to create `/internal/jobs/document_process.go`
   - Fetch file, call `gRPC.ParseAndEmbed()`, store chunks/embeddings, update status

2. **Handler Registration** - Modify `cmd/worker/main.go`
   - Register handler for `queue.TypeDocumentProcess`
   - Pass gRPC client, repository instances

3. **Upload → Queue Integration** - Modify `internal/service/upload.go`
   - Call `queueClient.EnqueueDocumentProcess()` after creating document

4. **Python `ParseAndEmbed()`** - Implement in Python worker
   - Parse document → chunks → embeddings → return ParseResponse

5. **Go Config** - Add gRPC address to config
   - `AIWorker.Address` env var

6. **Repository Check** - Verify `ChunkRepository` and `EmbeddingRepository` exist

**Complete analysis saved to knowledge base** for detailed reference on signatures, payloads, and integration points.
```

55	// TextSearch performs a full-text search across chunks in a knowledge base.
56	func (s *SearchService) TextSearch(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID, query string, limit int) (*model.SearchResponse, error) {
57		q := sanitizeQuery(query)
58		if q == "" {
59			return &model.SearchResponse{Results: []model.ChunkWithRank{}, Total: 0}, nil
60		}
61		limit = clampLimit(limit)
62	
63		var results []model.ChunkWithRank
64		err := db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
65			var err error
66			results, err = s.repo.TextSearch(ctx, tx, orgID, kbID, q, limit)
67			return err
68		})
69		if err != nil {
70			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("failed to search chunks: " + err.Error())
71		}
72		results = lo.Ternary(results == nil, []model.ChunkWithRank{}, results)
73		return &model.SearchResponse{Results: results, Total: len(results)}, nil
74	}
75	
76	// TextSearchWithFilters performs a full-text search restricted to specific documents.
77	func (s *SearchService) TextSearchWithFilters(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID, query string, docIDs []string, limit int) (*model.SearchResponse, error) {
78		q := sanitizeQuery(query)
79		if q == "" {
80			return &model.SearchResponse{Results: []model.ChunkWithRank{}, Total: 0}, nil
81		}
82		limit = clampLimit(limit)
83	
84		if len(docIDs) == 0 {
85			return s.TextSearch(ctx, orgID, kbID, q, limit)
86		}
87	
88		var results []model.ChunkWithRank
89		err := db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
90			var err error
91			results, err = s.repo.TextSearchWithFilters(ctx, tx, orgID, kbID, q, docIDs, limit)
92			return err
93		})
94		if err != nil {
95			return nil, apierror.NewInternal("failed to search chunks: " + err.Error())
96		}
97		results = lo.Ternary(results == nil, []model.ChunkWithRank{}, results)
98		return &model.SearchResponse{Results: results, Total: len(results)}, nil
99	}
100	
101	// HybridSearch performs a combined vector similarity + BM25 keyword search,
102	// merging results via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF). The embedding parameter
103	// should be pre-computed by the caller (e.g., via an embedding API).
104	//
105	// Both retrieval strategies run inside the same RLS-scoped transaction.
106	// Results are ranked by fused RRF score in descending order.
107	func (s *SearchService) HybridSearch(ctx context.Context, orgID, kbID string, query string, embedding []float32, topK int) (*model.HybridSearchResponse, error) {
108		q := sanitizeQuery(query)
109		topK = clampLimit(topK)
110	
111		// Both retrievers use an expanded candidate set so RRF has enough signal.
112		candidateK := topK * 3
113		if candidateK > maxSearchLimit {
114			candidateK = maxSearchLimit
115		}
116	
117		var vectorResults, bm25Results []model.HybridSearchResult
118	
119		err := db.WithOrgID(ctx, s.pool, orgID, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
120			var vErr, bErr error
121	
122			// Vector search (always run if embedding is provided).
123			if len(embedding) > 0 {
124				vectorResults, vErr = s.repo.VectorSearch(ctx, tx, kbID, embedding, candidateK)
125				if vErr != nil {
126					return vErr
127				}
128			}
129	
```

13	// SearchRepository handles full-text and vector search queries against the
14	// chunks and embeddings tables. All operations use a pgx.Tx with org_id set
15	// for RLS enforcement.
16	type SearchRepository struct {
17		pool *pgxpool.Pool
18	}
19	
20	// NewSearchRepository creates a new SearchRepository.
21	func NewSearchRepository(pool *pgxpool.Pool) *SearchRepository {
22		return &SearchRepository{pool: pool}
23	}
24	
25	const searchColumns = `c.id, c.org_id, c.knowledge_base_id, c.document_id, c.source_id,
26		c.content, c.chunk_index, c.token_count, c.page_number, c.heading, c.chunk_type, c.created_at`
27	
28	func scanChunkWithRank(rows pgx.Rows) ([]model.ChunkWithRank, error) {
29		var results []model.ChunkWithRank
30		for rows.Next() {
31			var cr model.ChunkWithRank
32			if err := rows.Scan(
33				&cr.ID, &cr.OrgID, &cr.KnowledgeBaseID, &cr.DocumentID, &cr.SourceID,
34				&cr.Content, &cr.ChunkIndex, &cr.TokenCount, &cr.PageNumber, &cr.Heading,
35				&cr.ChunkType, &cr.CreatedAt, &cr.Rank, &cr.Highlight,
36			); err != nil {
37				return nil, fmt.Errorf("scanChunkWithRank: %w", err)
38			}
39			results = append(results, cr)
40		}
41		return results, rows.Err()
42	}
43	
44	// TextSearch performs a full-text search across content and heading using
45	// ts_rank_cd for relevance ranking and ts_headline for snippet generation.
46	func (r *SearchRepository) TextSearch(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx, orgID, kbID, query string, limit int) ([]model.ChunkWithRank, error) {
47		sql := `SELECT ` + searchColumns + `,
48			ts_rank_cd(to_tsvector('english', coalesce(c.heading, '') || ' ' || c.content), q) AS rank,
49			ts_headline('english', c.content, q,
50				'MaxWords=35, MinWords=15, ShortWord=3, HighlightAll=FALSE') AS highlight
51		FROM chunks c, plainto_tsquery('english', $1) q
52		WHERE c.org_id = $2 AND c.knowledge_base_id = $3
53			AND to_tsvector('english', coalesce(c.heading, '') || ' ' || c.content) @@ q
54		ORDER BY rank DESC
55		LIMIT $4`
56	
57		rows, err := tx.Query(ctx, sql, query, orgID, kbID, limit)
58		if err != nil {
59			return nil, fmt.Errorf("SearchRepository.TextSearch: %w", err)
60		}
